A recrystallised thin film of the CT-996 drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The needle-shaped,
radiating crystals are known as ‘acicular spherulites’. The colours,
shapes and thermal behaviour of these structures reveal important
information about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts
its stability, processability and bioavailability.

1. 2025 in brief

2025 was a year of meaningful progress for Roche. We made a significant
positive impact on patients’ lives while reducing the environmental
footprint of our products and operations and contributing to
strengthening resilient healthcare systems around the world.


1.1 Letter to shareholders                                          10
1.2 2025 highlights                                                 16




The zosurabalpin drug substance recrystallising from a solution,
viewed using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The image
shows hundreds of prismatic single crystals. Analysing their size,
shape and growth behaviour provides critical information for
drug product stability.

The digital path: how digital technology and AI
are redefining pathology and disease detection
Pathologists are now wielding AI-powered tools to see beyond the human eye,
ensuring maximum precision in disease detection and treatment.


Pathology is a powerful yet often overlooked            digital transformation. Dr Lozano attests to the
field of medicine, where meticulous analysis of cells   immediate impact: “In our experience, digital
and tissues under a microscope unlocks crucial          pathology increases the pathology’s productivity.
diagnoses and shapes treatment plans for patients.      We save time.”
But as Dr Dolores Lozano Escario, Co-Director of
Pathology at Clínica Universidad de Navarra, aptly      Beyond efficiency, digital pathology fosters
puts it, “We are living an exciting moment – the        unprecedented collaboration. “With digital
shift to digital pathology.”                            pathology we can share cases with more expert
                                                        pathologists, be more accurate and ask in some
A new era of precision and speed                        cases, especially in difficult cases, for help.” This
For decades, the pathology process was largely          means a patient in Pamplona can benefit from
manual. Tissue samples, meticulously prepared           the expertise of a specialist across the globe,
on glass slides, were the pathologist’s universe.       simply by sharing a digital image.
Obtaining a second opinion involved physically
transporting these delicate slides, a process           The power of AI – beyond the human eye
prone to delays and even damage. “Before digital        Perhaps the most transformative aspect is the
pathology, seeking a second opinion meant               integration of AI and image analysis algorithms.
physically sending the samples for consultation,”       AI-based digital tools provide “more specific
Dr Lozano explains. “There was a risk of the            information that doesn’t rely anymore on the
delicate slides being lost or damaged.”                 subjectivity of our eyes on the microscope,”
                                                        notes Dr Lozano. AI can detect subtle patterns
Today, high-resolution scanners convert these           and anomalies that are sometimes invisible to the
physical slides into digital images, accessible         human eye, offering deeper insights and more
instantly from anywhere. “Pathology is undergoing       precise diagnoses for patients. “I hope that in

digitalisation,” says Lee Lehman-Becker, Head           things our eyes are unable to detect.”

transformation is fuelled by the need to provide        This blend of human expertise and digital

diagnostic results, regardless of their geography       making them smarter, more accurate and globally
and access to medical professionals and                 connected. As Dr Lozano states, “Everything that
specialists.”                                           helps us make a better diagnosis will improve patient
                                                        care and the healthcare system overall.”

2. About Roche

For more than 125 years, we have taken on some of the most
complex challenges in healthcare, listening and responding
to the ever-changing needs of people around the world.

A recrystallised thin film of the risdiplam drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The radiating structures
with different colour patterns are known as ‘spherulites’. The
colours, shapes and thermal behaviour of these structures reveal
important information about the drug’s crystal form, which directly
impacts its stability, processability and bioavailability.

                      2.1 Our purpose


                      Doing now what patients need next
                      We believe it is urgent to deliver medical solutions right now – even as we
                      develop innovations for the future. We are passionate about transforming
                      patients’ lives. We are courageous in both decision and action. And we
                      believe that good business means a better world.


                      That is why we come to work each day. We commit ourselves to scientific
                      rigour, unassailable ethics and access to medical innovations for all.
                      We do this today to build a better tomorrow.


                      We are proud of who we are, what we do and how we do it. We are many,
                      working as one across functions, across companies and across the world.

                      We are Roche.

When memory fades, science brings hope
Alzheimer’s takes what makes us who we are. Our science aims to protect the
memories that connect us and give families more time together.

father came like whispers. A giant bag of sugar, big                         what if a diagnosis did more than identify a disease?

he forgot the bank password he’d used for decades,                           even prevent it?
it felt different.

habits, convinced Silvia that it was time to see the                         biomarker tests and medicines currently in research
doctor. Cognitive tests and a brain scan confirmed                           and development, might work together to slow, stop
the news she feared. Her 88-year-old dad was in                              or even prevent progression.
the early stages of Alzheimer’s. Suddenly, he wasn’t
just her father struggling with memory. He was                              “Our ultimate goal is to give people living with

A recrystallised thin film of the risdiplam drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The vibrant, radiating
patterns are known as ‘spherulites’. The colours, shapes and
thermal behaviour of these structures reveal important information
about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts its stability,
processability and bioavailability.

A recrystallised thin film of the risdiplam drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The radiating pattern
at the bottom is known as a ‘spherulite’, while the upper part of
the image shows ‘bladed’ polycrystalline structures. The colours,
shapes and thermal behaviour of these structures reveal important
information about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts its
stability, processability and bioavailability.

2. General information

Roche has prepared this Sustainability Report in accordance with
the Swiss Code of Obligations and with reference to the European
Sustainability Reporting Standards. This reflects our commitment
to transparency, ensuring that essential sustainability information
across environmental, social, governance and human rights
aspects is fully integrated into our reporting.

2.2 Sustainability governance                                         77

2.4 Double materiality assessment                                     84




A recrystallised thin film of the fenebrutinib drug substance,
viewed using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The vibrant,
radiating pattern is known as ‘spherulite’. The colours, shapes and
thermal behaviour of this structure reveals important information
about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts its stability,
processability and bioavailability.

3. Environment

Respect for the environment has always been a priority for Roche. We
are accelerating our efforts to address our impacts across the company
and are actively collaborating within our value chain and industry to
make a positive contribution to the health of people and our planet.

A recrystallised thin film of the risdiplam drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The vibrant, radiating
patterns are known as ‘spherulites’. The colours, shapes and
thermal behaviour of these structures reveal important information
about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts its stability,
processability and bioavailability.

2 Scope 3 categories 8, 10, 13, 14 have been assessed and confirmed to have no material impact.
3 Purchased goods and services and capital goods emissions have been restated for 2024 to reflect spend corrections made following year-end and corrections of activity-based
   emissions factors.
4 Upstream transportation and distribution figures have been restated following a migration to a new calculation and reporting platform. Distribution locations previously reported using
   estimates are now included within the activity-based measurement boundary. Data for 2022–2024 have been adjusted.
5 Business travel emissions factors have been updated in line with latest UK-Government-published data. COVID-19-impacted load factors previously used for passenger kilometre data in
   2023–2024 have been updated to reflect normal loading, with figures restated. COVID-19-impacted load factors were applied for 2022.
6 Inclusion criteria for non-permanent employees have been revised and prior-year values have been restated on a consistent basis.
7 Downstream transportation and distribution figures have been restated for 2022–2024. The product weight used in the calculation has been adjusted to reflect improved data following
   the transition of distribution locations from estimates to the activity-based measurement boundary.
8 End-of-life treatment of sold products emissions have been restated for 2024 to reflect the corrections of activity-based emissions factors.

3.3 Water

Access to a clean and reliable water supply is a        The Roche water programme includes managing
fundamental human right and is crucial for producing    phosphorus and nitrogen in wastewater discharges,
our medicines and diagnostic solutions. We              lowering consumption in high-risk areas and ensuring
acknowledge that effective water stewardship is vital   that wastewater treatment meets stringent standards.
for our operations and to manage potential negative     We align our approach with recognised frameworks
impacts on local communities and the environment.       such as the Science Based Targets Network (SBTN)
                                                        and the Alliance for Water Stewardship (AWS).
Our approach focuses on reducing water consumption,
preventing pollution and protecting ecosystems.

                      3.5 Product sustainability

                       Product-related environmental performance has                trade-offs (e.g. development, manufacturing,
                       become a business imperative, shaping market                 packaging, distribution, use and disposal) and are
                       access, procurement decisions and stakeholder                informed by evolving market expectations and
                       trust. This growing importance is driven by                  legislative trends such as the EU Packaging and
                       increasing demands from customers, regulators and            Packaging Waste Regulation.
                       healthcare systems. While product sustainability
                       was not identified as one of our material topics in          We work closely with internal stakeholders
                       the DMA, advancing product sustainability is a core          across research and development, manufacturing,
                       part of our sustainability strategy and supports our         procurement and regulatory affairs, as well as
                       efforts on climate action, responsible resource use          with suppliers, customers and industry associations,
                       and the transition to a circular economy.                    to understand emerging requirements and
                                                                                    innovation opportunities. This collaboration
                       Our approach is designed to minimise resource use,           allows us to anticipate regulatory changes, align
                       emissions and waste across the entire value chain            with customer expectations and identify ways to
                       while strictly maintaining patient safety and product        enhance resource efficiency and circularity in
                       efficacy. We integrate life cycle thinking into our          the healthcare value chain.
                       decision-making, treating sustainability as a core
                       factor alongside traditional metrics like cost, speed
                       and quality. This means environmental impacts are            Policies
                       assessed across the product life cycle – from early
                       design and manufacturing to packaging, distribution,         Product sustainability is anchored in the Roche
                       use and end-of-life disposal.                                Position on Product Stewardship, which is guided
                                                                                    by the principle that all involved stakeholders are
                                                                                    responsible for minimising the negative impacts on
                       Impacts, risks and opportunities                             people and the environment. The policy requires
                                                                                    us to integrate green chemistry and eco-design
                       Guided by life cycle thinking and supported                  into product development through internal training,
                       by internal expertise in eco-design and green                guidance and expert workshops. Products undergo
                       chemistry, we assess the environmental footprint             sustainability performance checks, including life
                       of our products across all stages of the life cycle,         cycle assessments, so that their environmental
                       from raw-material sourcing to end of life.                   profile can be evaluated and improved.

                       Product sustainability risks and opportunities are           We also provide consumers and end users with

                       scanning. These processes consider impacts and               support circular-economy principles.

4. Social

We are committed to advancing equitable health outcomes by
maximising access to our innovative solutions for patients worldwide.
We also foster a thriving work environment where our people are
valued, safe and empowered to consistently deliver their best work.

A recrystallised thin film of the fenebrutinib drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The distinct bands of colour
and the ‘brushed’ textures of different size and direction show the
growth of crystals with different kinetics at different temperatures.
The colours, shapes and thermal behaviour of these structures reveal
important information about the drug’s crystal form, which directly
impacts its stability, processability and bioavailability.

5. Governance

Our governance processes uphold high standards of business ethics
and integrity, ensuring a strong foundation for our strategic priorities.
This includes diligently managing risks and enabling transparent
reporting on our sustainability progress.


5.1 Corporate culture and business ethics and integrity               138




A recrystallised thin film of the risdiplam drug substance, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The vibrant, radiating
patterns are known as ‘spherulites’. The colours, shapes and
thermal behaviour of these structures reveal important information
about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts its stability,
processability and bioavailability.

                           5.1 Corporate culture and business ethics and integrity

6. Human rights

We respect and proactively support human rights. We are
committed to upholding human rights across our corporate
strategy, governance and daily operations as well as
throughout our value chain.


6.1 Human rights                                                             144




A recrystallised thin film of the HER2 tyrosine kinase inhibitor, viewed
using hot-stage polarised light microscopy. Long, blade-like crystals
erupt from a central point where the first nucleus originated. The colours,
shapes and thermal behaviour of these structures reveal important
information about the drug’s crystal form, which directly impacts its
stability, processability and bioavailability.

A recrystallised thin film of the risdiplam drug substance, viewed using
hot-stage polarised light microscopy. The distinct bands of colour and
the ‘brushed’ textures show the growth of crystals in an amorphous
phase. The colours, shapes and thermal behaviour of these structures
reveal important information about the drug’s crystal form, which
directly impacts its stability, processability and bioavailability.

      equity securities in consideration of reduction of value due to additional blocking period of four years; reduced market value: 79.209%).
22	Calculation of RSUs value 2025: number of RSUs: 2,989, multiplied by grant value of CHF 294.40 (NES closing price at grant date on 27 March 2025) per RSU (calculation of value
      of non-voting equity securities / shares in consideration of reduction of value due to blocking period of ten years; reduced market value: 55.839%).
      Calculation of RSUs value 2024: number of RSUs: 3,442, multiplied by grant value of CHF 232.40 (NES closing price at grant date on 14 March 2024) per RSU (calculation of value
      of non-voting equity securities / shares in consideration of reduction of value due to blocking period of ten years; reduced market value: 55.839%).
23	Includes an annual expense allowance of CHF 30,000 (2024: CHF 30,000), payments for tax consulting services of CHF 28,306 (2024: CHF 10,679), ordinary family, children and
      education allowances of CHF 13,110 (2024: CHF 13,110) and other payments of CHF 1,719. Additionally, employer contribution to AHV/IV/ALV of CHF 384,932 (2024: CHF 350,481)
      was paid that does not form part of remuneration.

This image shows the zosurabalpin drug
substance recrystallising from a solution,
viewed using hot-stage polarised light
microscopy. The image reveals countless
individual crystallites (small crystals).
Analysing their size, shape and growth
behaviour provides critical information
for drug product stability.
